Iteration 1: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Critical: Goal Absorption Takes 3-5 Minutes

When a goal is scored, the price does NOT jump instantly to its new fair value. Markets absorb goals gradually over 3-5 minutes.

**When recent_events shows a goal in the last 1-4 minutes, the absorption is likely incomplete.** Check the candlesticks:
- If they show a sharp jump in the last 2-5 bars (the initial reaction), continuation is likely
- Typical continuation: +2c to +4c in the direction the goal favors
- Use half_width 1.5-2.5c to cover absorption uncertainty

Example failure (Example 3): KXEREDIVISIEGAME at 12:35Z, 0-0 at 5', mid 0.095. Predicted +0.1c ± 0.5c. A goal occurred during the window; realized +12.0c. The forecast missed both the event and the absorption pattern.

**When you see recent substitutions but no goals, and the score is tied or close, a goal may be imminent.** Widen half_width to 2-3c rather than predicting zero movement.

## Physical Bounds: Prices Cannot Exceed [0, 100]

A contract at 0.5c can only fall 0.5c or rise a few cents. A contract at 96c can only rise 4c or fall.

Your delta_cents must respect:
- If mid < 5c: maximum realistic upward delta is ~2-3c, maximum downward is ~mid
- If mid > 95c: maximum realistic upward delta is ~(100 - mid), maximum downward is ~3-5c

Example 2: mid 0.005 (0.5c), predicted -0.44c. This would imply a mid of -0.435c, which is impossible. When the market is already at the floor, predict zero or tiny upward drift, not further decline.

## Half-Width Must Match True Uncertainty

Half_width_cents is the radius of genuine uncertainty about where the price will be. It is not a by-product of your probability distribution unless you make it one—it is a choice.

**The default 0.5c half-width is too narrow for most situations.** Use it only when:
- Score is stable (no goal in last 5+ minutes)
- Clock is not in critical decay zone (not final 20 minutes of a close game)
- Candlesticks show no sharp moves in last 10-15 minutes
- Book is liquid and quiet

Widen half_width when:
- Recent goal still absorbing (last 1-4 minutes): 1.5-2.5c
- Draw contract in final 20 minutes (time decay uncertainty): 1.0-2.0c
- High volatility (candlesticks show 5c+ swings recently): 2.0-3.0c
- Early in match with substitutions but no goals yet: 2.0-3.0c (goal imminent risk)
- You predict directional movement but cannot rule out the opposite: widen enough to cover both

Example failures:
- Example 2: predicted -0.4c ± 0.5c, realized +0.0c (market at floor, couldn't fall further)
- Example 4: predicted -0.0c ± 0.5c, realized -2.0c (missed Columbus goal absorption)
- Example 6: predicted +0.1c ± 0.5c, realized +0.0c (false signal, but width was reasonable for stable market)
- Example 7: predicted +0.2c ± 0.5c, realized -3.0c (missed LAFC goal, absorption went opposite direction)

**If you predict near-zero movement (|delta| < 0.5c), you are claiming high confidence the market will stay put.** Only do this when the evidence strongly supports stability. Otherwise, either predict the directional driver or widen to 2-3c.

## Time Decay is Real and Directional

A "will X happen" contract decays toward zero as the clock runs down without X occurring:
- Draw contracts decay as the game approaches 90' with no additional goals changing the outcome
- "Team X to win" decays if Team X is losing and time is running out
- Early in the match (minute 5-30), time decay is negligible; focus on score state and goal absorption
- In final 20 minutes, time decay becomes significant: typically -0.5c to -2.0c per five minutes depending on clock and score

Time decay is predictable, not noise. Factor it into delta_cents when appropriate.

## Most Five-Minute Windows Are Stable

When the score is stable, no goal in the last 4+ minutes, no event imminent, and the book is quiet, predicting zero movement with 0.4-0.5c half-width is correct.

But distinguish:
- **Truly stable**: 1-1 at 35' with last goal at 28' (7 minutes ago), book quiet for 10+ bars → predict 0 ± 0.4c
- **False stable**: 1-1 at 55' but last goal at 52' (3 minutes ago) and candlesticks show a jump → predict continuation +2c ± 1.8c
- **Imminent change**: 0-0 at 15' with recent substitutions and high volume → widen to 2.5c even if predicting zero delta

## Check Recent Events for Goals You Might Miss

Recent_events lists goals, cards, substitutions, and delays. **Always check for goals in the last 5 minutes.** If you find one:
1. Check candlesticks to see if the initial jump has happened (large volume spike, mid moved 5-20c)
2. If yes, predict continuation in the same direction: typically +2c to +4c over next 5 minutes
3. Use half_width 1.5-2.5c to cover absorption uncertainty
4. If the goal was very recent (last 1-2 minutes), the continuation may be larger: +3c to +6c

Example 1 success: KXLIGAMXGAME at 04:15Z, 0-0 at 75'. Candlesticks showed a jump from ~0.50 to ~0.56 in the last few bars (goal absorption starting). Predicted +0.3c ± 1.3c. Realized -1.5c (the goal was absorbed in the opposite direction than expected, but the wide half-width covered it). Both sides of the quote were hit, suggesting the width was appropriate.

## Summary of Rules

1. **Always check recent_events for goals in the last 5 minutes.** If found and candlesticks show an initial jump, predict continuation (+2c to +4c) with half_width 1.5-2.5c.

2. **Respect physical bounds.** If mid < 5c, don't predict negative delta below -mid. If mid > 95c, don't predict positive delta above +(100 - mid).

3. **Default 0.5c half-width is only for genuinely stable quiet markets.** Use 1.5-3.0c when there is directional risk, recent goals, high volatility, or imminent events.

4. **Predict zero movement only when you have strong evidence the market will stay put.** If uncertain, widen your half-width to avoid being run over.

5. **Time decay is directional and predictable** in the final 20-30 minutes of a match when the score state supports it.

6. **When the market is at extremes (< 5c or > 95c), it is more likely to mean-revert or stay put than to continue trending.** Adjust your forecast accordingly.

Iteration 5: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Goal Absorption Takes 3-5 Minutes

When a goal is scored, the price does NOT jump instantly to its new fair value. Markets take 3-5 minutes to fully absorb a goal. 

**Example from feedback (Example 3):** KXMLSGAME-26SEP12STLMIN-STL at minute 45' shows St. Louis 3-1 Minnesota. Recent_events shows a goal at 37' - eight minutes ago. The candlesticks show the price was stable around 0.485 through minute 44, then crashed from 0.485 to 0.355 at minute 45 (the halftime whistle), then bounced to 0.435. At the forecast time (start of second half), the mid is 0.685. Five minutes later it reached 0.875 (+19c). This is NOT a stable situation - the goal at 37' in first half, combined with the halftime break, means the market is still finding fair value in the opening minutes of the second half.

**When a goal occurred in the last 5-8 minutes, especially if there was a halftime break after it, predict continuation of the absorption process.** The market may still have 10-20 cents to move as traders reassess.

Check recent_events carefully:
- Goal in last 2 minutes + candlesticks show sharp jump: predict +2 to +4c continuation, half_width 1.5-2.5c
- Goal 3-5 minutes ago + price has moved but still volatile: predict +1 to +3c continuation, half_width 1.0-2.0c
- Goal 6-8 minutes ago + halftime or other disruption: check candlesticks for whether absorption completed; if not, predict continuation
- Goal >10 minutes ago: absorption likely complete unless candlesticks show ongoing drift

## Half-Width Must Reflect True Uncertainty

Your half_width_cents is the radius of genuine uncertainty. The feedback shows systematic underestimation of uncertainty:

**Most failures had half_width = 0.5c but realized moves of 2-5 cents outside the band.**

Rules for half_width:
- **Default should be 1.0-1.5c, not 0.5c.** Markets are more volatile than the current harness assumes.
- Genuinely stable situation (0-0 at minute 15, no goals in last 10 minutes, quiet book, low volume): 0.5-0.8c
- Recent goal still absorbing (last 2-5 minutes): 2.0-3.0c to cover continuation risk
- Start of second half after first-half goal: 2.0-3.5c (teams reset, market reassesses)
- Draw contract in final 20 minutes: 1.5-2.5c for time decay uncertainty
- High-volume volatile book (candlesticks show 5c+ swings recently): 2.5-3.5c
- Early in match (minutes 1-15) with no events yet: 1.0-1.5c (goals can happen any time)

**If you predict near-zero movement (|delta| < 0.5c), you are claiming high confidence the market will stay put. Use half_width 0.5c ONLY when you have strong evidence: no goals in 10+ minutes, low volume, stable candlesticks, no imminent events.**

## Distinguish Truly Stable from False Stable

Many examples that looked "stable" had goals during the forecast window or were still absorbing recent goals:

- Example 1: 0-0 at minute 25, recent event only a yellow card at 20', but mid was 0.775 (heavily favoring Monaco). Five minutes later mid dropped to 0.750 (-2.5c). This is natural drift on a quiet book, not instability. **Predict 0 ± 0.5c was wrong only because half_width was too narrow; should be 0 ± 1.0c for a thin book.**

- Example 3: 3-1 at start of second half after a goal at 37'. This is NOT stable. Predict continuation.

- Example 7: 0-1 at minute 55', no recent goal, two yellow cards. Market had moved from 0.365 to 0.44 to 0.665 over the prior 35 minutes (huge volatility), suggesting a goal had occurred earlier. Realized -0.5c. **High recent volatility means wide half_width even if no event in last 5 minutes.**

**Truly stable:** Score unchanged for 10+ minutes, candlesticks flat, volume low, no recent cards or dangerous play.

**False stable:** Recent goal not fully absorbed, high volatility in candlesticks, start of period after goal, or thin book that can drift.

## Time Decay is Real But Gradual

A "Team X to win" contract decays toward zero as time runs out if Team X is not ahead. A "draw" contract decays as the clock winds down without new goals. But this decay is gradual on a 5-minute horizon:

- Minutes 1-45: negligible time decay, focus on score state and goal absorption
- Minutes 46-70: very slow decay, ~0.1-0.3c per 5 minutes
- Minutes 71-85: moderate decay, ~0.3-0.8c per 5 minutes
- Minutes 86-90+: fast decay, ~1-3c per 5 minutes

**Do not over-predict time decay.** Example 6 at minute 65 in a 1-1 draw: predicted +0.1c, realized 0c. Correct prediction, but half_width 0.5c was too narrow (should be 1.0-1.5c to cover uncertainty).

## Physical Bounds at Price Extremes

A contract at 0.5c can only fall to 0c (max -0.5c) or rise. A contract at 96c can only rise to 100c (max +4c) or fall. Respect these bounds:
- If mid < 5c: max upward move ~3-4c, max downward move ~mid
- If mid > 95c: max upward move ~(100 - mid), max downward move ~4-5c
- If mid between 5c and 95c: typical max move ±5c on 5-minute horizon, ±10-20c only when absorbing a fresh goal

## When to Predict Zero Movement

Predict delta_cents = 0 when:
- Score stable for 10+ minutes
- No goal in last 10 minutes
- Candlesticks show flat mid for 10+ bars
- Volume low and declining
- Clock not in final 15 minutes (time decay minimal)
- Book not thin (spread < 2c, consistent volume)

**But even when predicting zero, use half_width 0.8-1.5c unless you have overwhelming evidence of stability.**

## Summary of Fixes from Feedback

1. **Default half_width is 1.0-1.5c, not 0.5c.** Use 0.5c only for genuinely quiet, stable markets with strong evidence. Use 2-3c when there is recent goal absorption, high volatility, or start of a new period.

2. **Goal absorption continues 3-5 minutes after the event, longer if there was a halftime break.** Check recent_events and candlesticks together. If a goal occurred in last 5-8 minutes and candlesticks show ongoing movement, predict continuation.

3. **Start of second half after a first-half goal is not stable.** The market is still finding fair value. Predict continuation and use wide half_width (2-3c).

4. **High volatility in recent candlesticks means wide half_width even if no goal in last 5 minutes.** Example 7 showed 30c of movement in prior 35 minutes; this is not a stable book.

5. **Predicting zero movement is correct only when you have strong evidence the market will stay put.** If uncertain, either predict the directional driver or use wide half_width (1.5-3c) to avoid getting run over.

6. **Time decay is gradual.** Do not over-predict decay except in final 10 minutes of a match.

7. **Thin books drift.** If spread is wide or volume is sporadic, even a "stable" situation should have half_width 1.0-1.5c to cover single-order drift.

Iteration 12: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Critical: Goal Absorption Takes 3-5 Minutes

When a goal is scored, the price does NOT jump instantly to its new fair value. Markets absorb goals gradually over 3-5 minutes. The feedback shows:

- Example 7: 0-0 at 25' in Monaco vs Marseille, mid at 0.675 (Monaco to win). Price rose +2.5c over the next 5 minutes. A goal likely occurred during this window and the market was absorbing it.
- Example 8: 0-0 at 25' in DC United vs Nashville, mid at 0.270 (DC United to win). Price jumped +4.5c over the next 5 minutes. A goal occurred and the market took the full window to absorb it.

**When a goal happens during your forecast window, the price can move 2-5 cents in the direction of the scoring team's win probability.** Early in a match (minute 15-30), goals are the primary driver of large moves.

Check recent_events carefully. If a goal is listed in the last 2-3 minutes and candlesticks show a recent jump, predict continuation in the same direction (typically +2 to +4 cents) with wider half-width (1.5-2.5c).

## Half-Width Must Match Actual Uncertainty

Your half_width_cents is the radius of genuine uncertainty—the distance from your delta within which you are confident the realized price will land. The feedback shows systematic failures from half-widths that are too narrow:

- Examples 1, 2, 3, 4: Predicted near-zero movement (delta ≈ 0c) with 0.5c half-width. Realized outcomes were 0c, which scored but provided no information about whether the half-width was appropriate.
- Example 7: Predicted +0.2c ± 0.8c, realized +2.5c → outside the band.
- Example 8: Predicted -0.6c ± 0.6c, realized +4.5c → outside the band.

**The default 0.5c half-width is only appropriate for genuinely stable markets: 0-0 or 1-1 draw in first half, no recent goals, no recent events, candlesticks flat for 10+ minutes, low volume.**

Rules for half_width:
- **Stable, quiet market** (0-0 at 15' with no action): 0.3-0.5c
- **Recent goal still absorbing** (goal in last 2-3 minutes): 1.5-2.5c to cover continuation risk
- **Early in match with potential for goals** (0-0 at 15-30'): 1.5-3c because a goal could occur during your 5-minute window
- **Second half, score differential, time decay active**: 1-2c for decay uncertainty
- **High volatility book** (candlesticks show 3c+ moves in last 10 minutes): 2-3c
- **Wide spread or thin book** (spread ≥ 2c, low volume): widen by 0.5-1c

**If you cannot confidently rule out a 3-5 cent move in either direction, your half-width must be at least 3c.** Getting run over (realized price outside your quote) is far more costly than standing a wide quote that doesn't trade.

## Distinguish True Stability from False Stability

Most five-minute windows don't move, but you must distinguish:

1. **True stability**: 1-0 at 60', no recent events, candlesticks flat at 0.35 ± 0.01c for 15 minutes, low volume → predict 0c ± 0.4c
2. **False stability before a goal**: 0-0 at 20', candlesticks show 5c moves in last 20 minutes, volume picking up → predict 0c ± 3c (you don't know *when* the goal will come, but one is likely)
3. **False stability after a goal**: 1-0 at 25' but goal was at 23', candlesticks show recent 10c jump → predict continuation +2c ± 2c

The feedback shows Examples 7 and 8 failed because early-match 0-0 scorelines with no obvious recent events can still see goals *during* the 5-minute forecast window. At minute 20-30 in a 0-0 game, unless the book is dead quiet (volume near zero, no trades, candlesticks completely flat), widen your half-width to 2-3c.

## Time Decay is Real But Only Matters Late

A "will X happen" contract decays toward zero as the clock runs down without X occurring. But:
- **Before minute 60**: time decay is negligible; focus on score state and goal absorption
- **Minute 60-75**: decay begins to matter, typically 0.5-1c per 5 minutes on a draw contract
- **Minute 75-90**: decay accelerates, 1-2c per 5 minutes on a draw contract, faster on "team to win" when losing

For a draw contract at 0-0 in minute 70+, predict gradual decay (−0.5 to −1c) but widen your half-width to 1.5-2c because a goal could still occur.

## Respect Physical Bounds at Price Extremes

A contract at 0.5c can only fall to 0c (max move: −0.5c). A contract at 96c can only rise to 100c (max move: +4c). Your delta_cents must respect these bounds:
- **If mid < 5c**: max realistic upward move ≈ 2-3c, max downward ≈ mid
- **If mid > 95c**: max realistic upward ≈ (100 − mid), max downward ≈ 3-5c

No failures here yet, but be aware.

## When to Predict Zero Movement

Predict delta_cents = 0 only when you have strong evidence the market will stay put:
- Score is stable (not 0-0 early, not a fresh lead)
- No goal in last 3 minutes
- Clock not in decay zone (before minute 60, or after but score differential is large)
- Candlesticks flat (< 1c range) for 10+ minutes
- Volume low or zero
- Spread is 1 tick and hasn't widened

Even then, use half_width 0.3-0.5c. If any of the above are false, either predict directional movement or widen significantly.

## Summary of What Moves Markets in Five Minutes

1. **A goal during the window** (Examples 7, 8): +2 to +5c toward winner. More likely early in match, especially 0-0 games.
2. **Absorbing a recent goal** (last 2-3 minutes): +2 to +4c continuation in same direction.
3. **Time decay** (minute 70+, draw or losing team): −0.5 to −2c depending on clock and score.
4. **Book mean reversion** (thin book that just jumped): −1 to −2c back toward prior level.
5. **Nothing** (stable score, no events, quiet book): 0c ± 0.3c.

Your task is to identify which regime applies, predict the central tendency, and set half_width wide enough to cover the genuine range of outcomes—including the possibility that a goal occurs during your forecast window even if none is visible in recent_events.

Iteration 18: Proposed new text for tools: market_quote, candlesticks, previous_trades, game_state, game_clock, minutes_since_goal, goal_absorption, state_summary, market_shock, tape_imbalance, settlement_countdown, price_velocity

Iteration 19: Selected program 2 score: 0.49532129166666694
Iteration 19: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened in the last 2-3 minutes

## Critical Pattern from Feedback: Goals Happen During Your Forecast Window

The feedback reveals a systematic failure mode: **when a goal occurs during your 5-minute forecast window, the price can move 5-23 cents instantly**, and your quote gets run over.

- Example 6: NE vs HOU at 55', mid 0.365. Five minutes later: 0.145 (−22.0c). A goal occurred during the window.
- Example 7: NCX vs ALA at 65', mid 0.440. Five minutes later: 0.210 (−23.0c). A goal occurred during the window.
- Example 8: BAR vs RVC at 15' (goal at 11'), mid 0.765. Five minutes later: 0.775 (+1.0c). Market still absorbing the recent goal.
- Example 2: LFC vs NFO at 75' (goal at 69'), mid 0.445. Five minutes later: 0.475 (+3.0c). Market still absorbing the recent goal.

**The critical distinction**: 
- **If a goal happened 2-3 minutes ago** (visible in recent_events), the market is absorbing it gradually: predict continuation of +2 to +4 cents.
- **If no recent goal is visible but one could occur during your window**, you cannot predict it, but you must widen your half-width to avoid getting run over.

## When a Goal Can Occur During Your Window

Goals are most likely:
1. **First half, 0-0 scoreline, minutes 10-45**: High risk. Games are open, both teams attacking.
2. **Second half, 0-0 or 1-1, minutes 45-70**: Moderate risk. Teams push for a winner.
3. **Second half, one-goal deficit, minutes 60-90**: High risk for the losing team. Desperate attack.
4. **Late in game, leading team protecting a lead**: Low risk. Game management mode.

Check candlesticks for recent volatility. If the book has moved 3+ cents in the last 10 minutes, goal risk is elevated.

## Half-Width Must Account for Goal Risk

The feedback shows catastrophic failures from narrow half-widths when goals occurred:
- Examples 6, 7: Predicted ~0c with 0.5c half-width. Goals during the window caused 22-23c moves. Total wipeout.

**New half-width rules:**

### No recent goal (last event >5 minutes ago):
- **First half, 0-0, minutes 10-40**: 3-5c minimum. A goal during your window would move the price 10-25c, and you cannot predict when it will come. Wide enough that if nothing happens, you don't trade, but if a goal comes, you're not ruined.
- **First half, 0-0, minutes 40-45**: 2-3c. Less time for a goal, but still possible.
- **Second half, 0-0 or 1-1, minutes 45-70**: 3-4c. Similar goal risk to early first half.
- **Second half, one-goal lead, minutes 60-80**: 2-3c. Both a goal and time decay are possible.
- **Second half, two-goal lead, any time**: 1-2c. Goal risk is low, but still non-zero.
- **Last 10 minutes, any scoreline**: 2-4c. Desperate goals and time decay both accelerate.

### Recent goal (in last 2-3 minutes, visible in recent_events):
- Predict continuation in the direction the price moved: +2 to +4c for the scoring team's win market.
- Half-width: 1.5-2.5c. You're riding momentum, but absorption can stall.

### Truly quiet market (rare):
- Candlesticks flat (< 0.5c range) for 15+ minutes.
- Volume near zero.
- Score differential is 2+ goals, or it's very early (before minute 10).
- Half-width: 0.3-0.5c.

**The core principle**: If you cannot confidently rule out a goal during your 5-minute window, your half-width must be wide enough that getting run over by a 10-20c move doesn't destroy your score. A 3-5c half-width that never trades is far better than a 0.5c half-width that gets annihilated by a goal.

## Time Decay is Secondary to Goal Risk

Time decay matters, but only in specific late-game situations:
- **Before minute 60**: Negligible. Focus entirely on goal risk and score state.
- **Minute 60-75, draw contract**: −0.5 to −1c per 5 minutes.
- **Minute 75-90, draw contract**: −1 to −2c per 5 minutes.
- **Minute 80-90, losing team's win contract**: −2 to −3c per 5 minutes as hope fades.

But even late, goal risk dominates. At 85' in a 0-0 game, predict −1c for decay but use 2-3c half-width because a goal could still come.

## Absorbing a Recent Goal

When recent_events shows a goal in the last 2-3 minutes:
1. Check candlesticks: did the price jump recently (5-15c move in last 3-5 minutes)?
2. If yes, predict continuation: +2 to +4c in the direction of the jump.
3. If the price hasn't moved yet, the market is about to absorb it: predict the full move (+10 to +20c for the scoring team).
4. Use half-width 1.5-2.5c. Absorption can be smooth or lumpy.

Example 2 (LFC vs NFO): goal at 69', at 75' the mid was 0.445, five minutes later 0.475. The market absorbed +3c during your window. This is predictable.

Example 8 (BAR vs RVC): goal at 11', at 15' the mid was 0.765 (already down from pre-goal ~0.89), five minutes later 0.775. Only +1c because most absorption was complete.

## When to Predict Zero Movement

Only when all of these are true:
1. **Score is stable**: Not 0-0 in first 60 minutes. Not a fresh lead. Not a one-goal game in final 20 minutes.
2. **No recent goal**: Last event in recent_events is >5 minutes ago.
3. **Candlesticks are dead flat**: < 0.5c range for 15+ minutes.
4. **Volume is low**: No large trades in recent_trades.
5. **Clock is not in danger zone**: Either before minute 60, or after but score differential is 2+.

Even then, use half-width 0.3-0.5c for micro-movements. If any of the above are false, either predict directional movement or widen significantly.

## Respect Physical Bounds at Price Extremes

- If mid < 5c: max realistic upward move ≈ 5-10c (a goal makes an unlikely outcome likely), max downward ≈ mid.
- If mid > 95c: max realistic upward ≈ (100 − mid), max downward ≈ 5-10c (a shock goal).

Example 1: Slovan Bratislava to win at 0.5c (PSG leading 3-0 at 55'). Correctly predicted 0c movement with 0.5c half-width. The game was over; even a Slovan goal wouldn't move it much.

## Summary: The Two Regimes

**Regime 1: Stable, predictable** (rare)
- Score differential 2+, or very early (before minute 10), or very late (after 85') with a lead.
- No recent goal (>5 minutes).
- Candlesticks flat for 15+ minutes.
- Predict: 0c ± 0.3-0.5c, or small decay (−0.5 to −1c) ± 0.5-1c if late.

**Regime 2: Goal risk active** (most of the time)
- First or second half, 0-0 or one-goal game.
- Minutes 10-80.
- Candlesticks show any volatility (>1c moves in last 10 minutes).
- Predict: 0c (you don't know when the goal comes) ± 3-5c (wide enough to survive if it does).
- If a recent goal (last 2-3 minutes): predict continuation +2 to +4c ± 1.5-2.5c.

**The feedback shows Examples 6 and 7 failed catastrophically because they used 0.5c half-width in Regime 2**. The harness got run over by goals it should have expected were possible. Don't make that mistake. When in doubt, go wide.

Iteration 20: No merge candidates found
Iteration 20: Selected program 1 score: 0.4957175833333333
Iteration 20: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Goal Absorption Takes 3-5 Minutes, Sometimes Longer

When a goal is scored, the price does NOT jump instantly to its new fair value. Markets take 3-5 minutes to fully absorb a goal, and sometimes longer if there's a halftime break or other disruption.

**A goal scored just before halftime may not be fully absorbed until several minutes into the second half.** Check recent_events carefully for goals in the prior 10 minutes, not just the current period.

When a goal occurred in the last 5-10 minutes:
- Check candlesticks to see if price has stabilized or is still moving
- If candlesticks show continued drift or volatility (5c+ moves in recent bars), predict continuation: +1 to +3c with half_width 2.0-3.0c
- If a halftime break occurred after the goal, the second half often sees continued absorption for 3-5 minutes
- Goal in last 2 minutes + sharp jump in candlesticks: predict +2 to +4c continuation, half_width 2.0-2.5c
- Goal 6-10 minutes ago: check if absorption is complete; if not, predict +1 to +2c, half_width 1.5-2.5c

## Half-Width Must Reflect True Uncertainty

Your half_width_cents is the radius of genuine uncertainty, not a by-product of the forecast's confidence. The feedback shows systematic underestimation of uncertainty.

**Most failures had half_width = 0.5c but realized moves of 2-5 cents outside the band.**

Rules for half_width (these are minimums, not targets):
- **Default should be 1.0-1.5c in most situations.** Markets are more volatile than narrow bands suggest.
- Genuinely stable situation (0-0 or tied at minute 15-30, no goals in last 10 minutes, candlesticks flat for 10+ bars, spread < 2c, steady volume): 0.8-1.2c
- Recent goal still absorbing (last 2-5 minutes): 2.0-3.0c
- Start of second half after first-half goal: 2.5-3.5c (market reassessing, high uncertainty)
- High volatility in recent candlesticks (5c+ swings in prior 5-10 bars): 2.5-3.5c, even if no recent event
- Draw contract in final 20 minutes: 1.5-2.5c for time decay uncertainty
- Early in match (minutes 1-20) with no score: 1.0-1.5c (goals can happen any time)
- Thin book (spread > 2c, sporadic volume): 1.0-2.0c to cover drift from single orders
- Mid price < 5c or > 95c: 1.0-2.0c (low liquidity at extremes)

**Use half_width 0.5c ONLY when you have overwhelming evidence of stability:** score unchanged 10+ minutes, candlesticks flat for 10+ bars, volume low and steady, spread tight, clock not in final 15 minutes, no recent cards or dangerous play.

## Goals Can Happen During the Forecast Window

Many examples showed goals occurring during the 5-minute forecast window, not just before it. A 0-0 game at minute 25 or 35 can see a goal in the next 5 minutes, causing a 20-30c move.

**Example 2:** 0-0 at minute 5 (early in match), predicted +0.0c with half_width 0.5c. Realized +2.5c. The market moved because the game state was evolving.

**Example 4:** 0-0 at minute 25, predicted -0.1c with half_width 0.5c. Realized +26.0c (a goal was scored during the forecast window).

When predicting "no move," you are claiming the market will stay put AND no goal will be scored in the next 5 minutes. This is only justified when:
- Score is stable and has been for 10+ minutes
- Candlesticks show the market has stabilized after any prior events
- Volume and spread indicate a liquid, stable book

**If you cannot rule out a goal in the next 5 minutes, use wide half_width (1.5-3.0c) to avoid being run over when the price does move.** It is better to quote wide and rarely trade than to quote narrow and get filled on the wrong side.

## Distinguish Truly Stable from False Stable

Many examples that looked "stable" were not:

**Truly stable:**
- Score unchanged for 10+ minutes
- Candlesticks flat (within 1c) for 10+ bars
- Volume low and steady
- Spread tight (< 2c)
- No recent goals, cards, or dangerous play
- Clock not in final 15 minutes (time decay minimal)

**False stable (use wide half_width):**
- Recent goal not fully absorbed (check candlesticks for continued drift)
- High volatility in recent candlesticks (5c+ swings), even if no event in last 5 minutes
- Start of period after a goal in prior period
- Thin book (wide spread, sporadic volume) that can drift on single orders
- Early in match (minutes 1-20) when goals are most likely
- Recent substitutions or cards indicating active play

**Example 1:** 1-1 at minute 35, no recent events, but candlesticks showed the mid had drifted from 0.185 to 0.105 over prior 10 minutes. This is not stable—it's a thin book drifting. Realized -3.5c. Should have predicted 0 ± 1.5c, not 0 ± 0.5c.

**Example 7:** 0-1 at minute 75, no recent goal, but candlesticks showed huge volatility (0.365 to 0.665 over prior 35 minutes). Realized +2.5c. Should have used half_width 2.5-3.5c to cover the high volatility.

## Time Decay is Real But Gradual

A "Team X to win" contract decays toward zero as time runs out if Team X is not ahead. A "draw" contract decays as the clock winds down without new goals. But this decay is gradual on a 5-minute horizon:

- Minutes 1-60: negligible time decay, focus on score state and goal absorption
- Minutes 61-75: very slow decay, ~0.1-0.3c per 5 minutes
- Minutes 76-85: moderate decay, ~0.3-0.8c per 5 minutes
- Minutes 86-90+: fast decay, ~1-3c per 5 minutes

**Do not over-predict time decay before minute 75.** The examples show time decay of -0.3c was too much at minute 65 in a 0-0 draw (Example 6).

## Physical Bounds at Price Extremes

A contract at 0.5c can only fall to 0c (max -0.5c) or rise. A contract at 96c can only rise to 100c (max +4c) or fall. Respect these bounds:
- If mid < 5c: max downward move is ~mid, upward moves can be large (20-30c if a goal)
- If mid > 95c: max upward move is ~(100 - mid), downward moves can be large (20-30c if opponent scores)
- If mid between 5c and 95c: typical max move ±5c on 5-minute horizon without a goal, ±20-40c if a goal is scored

**Low prices (< 5c) and high prices (> 95c) have lower liquidity and wider spreads.** Use half_width 1.0-2.0c minimum at extremes.

## Thin Books Drift

If the spread is wide (> 2c) or volume is sporadic, the mid can move 1-2c on a single order and drift back. This is not a directional forecast—it's noise.

**When the book is thin, use half_width 1.0-1.5c minimum, even if you predict zero movement.** You are covering single-order drift, not fundamental moves.

## When to Predict Zero Movement

Predict delta_cents = 0 when:
- Score stable for 10+ minutes
- No goal in last 10 minutes
- Candlesticks flat (within 1c) for 10+ bars
- Volume low and steady
- Spread tight (< 2c)
- Clock not in final 15 minutes (time decay minimal)
- No recent cards or substitutions
- You have strong evidence the market will stay put

**But even when predicting zero, use half_width 0.8-1.5c unless you have overwhelming evidence of stability.** The default is not 0.5c—it's 1.0-1.5c.

## Check Recent Events for Goals Beyond the Current Period

The recent_events field can show goals from the prior period (e.g., first half) that may still be absorbing in the current period (second half). Do not assume "no recent events" means "no recent goal"—check the full list.

**Example 3 (implied from context):** If a goal occurred at minute 37 in the first half and the forecast time is early in the second half (minute 46-50), the market may still be absorbing that goal. Predict continuation and use wide half_width (2.5-3.5c).

## Summary of Fixes from Feedback

1. **Default half_width is 1.0-1.5c, not 0.5c.** Use 0.5c only when you have overwhelming evidence the market will stay put. Use 2.0-3.5c when there is recent goal absorption, high volatility, or start of a new period.

2. **Goals can happen during the forecast window.** A 0-0 game can see a goal in the next 5 minutes, causing a 20-30c move. If you cannot rule out a goal, use wide half_width (1.5-3.0c).

3. **Check recent_events for goals in the prior 10 minutes, including goals from the prior period.** Absorption continues 3-5 minutes after the event, longer if there was a halftime break.

4. **High volatility in recent candlesticks means wide half_width, even if no goal in last 5 minutes.** Example 7 showed 30c of movement in prior 35 minutes; this is not a stable book. Use half_width 2.5-3.5c.

5. **Start of second half after a first-half goal is not stable.** The market is still finding fair value. Predict continuation and use wide half_width (2.5-3.5c).

6. **Thin books drift.** If spread is wide or volume is sporadic, use half_width 1.0-1.5c minimum to cover single-order noise.

7. **Time decay is gradual.** Do not over-predict decay before minute 75.

8. **Low prices (< 5c) and high prices (> 95c) are less liquid.** Use half_width 1.0-2.0c minimum.

9. **It is better to quote wide and rarely trade than to quote narrow and get run over.** A wide quote that doesn't trade scores zero, which is better than a narrow quote that gets filled on the wrong side.

Iteration 23: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Critical: Goal Absorption Takes 3-5 Minutes

When a goal is scored, the price does NOT jump instantly to its new fair value. Markets absorb goals gradually over 3-5 minutes.

**Check recent_events for goals in the last 5 minutes.** If you find one:
1. Check candlesticks to see if the initial jump has happened (a sharp move in the expected direction)
2. If the jump occurred, the absorption is likely NOT complete - predict continuation in the same direction
3. Typical continuation: +2 to +4 cents over the next 5 minutes
4. Use wider half-width (1.5-2.5c) to cover absorption uncertainty

Example 1 failure: At 5' minute mark, an own-goal at 3' caused the market to move from 0.455 to 0.255 in the preceding minutes. Predicted -0.3c, but market continued moving by +17c over the next 5 minutes as it fully absorbed the goal. The candlesticks showed high volume and the move was ongoing - this was classic incomplete absorption.

Example 8 failure: At 25' with 0-0 score, predicted -0.2c ± 0.5c, but market dropped -26c. Check if a goal occurred during the forecast window - if recent_events is empty at forecast time but the realized move is massive, a goal likely happened during the 5-minute window. You cannot predict goals before they happen, but you CAN recognize when absorption is incomplete.

## Check the Ticker to Know What You're Forecasting

The ticker encodes the question being asked. Common patterns:
- `TEAM1TEAM2-TEAM1`: "Will TEAM1 win?" (goes to 1 if TEAM1 wins, 0 otherwise)
- `TEAM1TEAM2-TEAM2`: "Will TEAM2 win?" (goes to 1 if TEAM2 wins, 0 otherwise)
- Draw contracts exist on some leagues

When the away team is leading and you're forecasting the home team to win, a goal FOR the away team should move the price DOWN (home team less likely to win). When forecasting the away team to win and the away team is already leading, a goal FOR them should move the price UP.

Example 1: `KXLIGAMXGAME-26AUG22PUESLA-PUE` asks "Will Puebla win?" At 5' the score is Puebla 0, Santos 1 (Santos leading). An own-goal by Puebla at 3' gave Santos the lead. This makes Puebla LESS likely to win, so the price (probability Puebla wins) should DROP. The market moved from 0.455 to 0.255 (down 20c), then continued to 0.425 (+17c from 0.255) over the next 5 minutes. This is absorption - the market was still processing the negative news for Puebla.

Always check: Does the direction of the candlestick move match what the goal means for THIS contract?

## Half-Width Must Match Actual Uncertainty

Your half_width_cents is the radius of genuine uncertainty about where the price will be. The feedback shows systematic underestimation:

**Default 0.5c half-width is too narrow in most situations.** Use it only for genuinely stable quiet markets with:
- No goals in the last 5 minutes
- No recent events suggesting imminent score changes
- Candlesticks showing minimal movement (< 2c range in last 15 minutes)
- Low volume, stable mid

When there is directional risk, use wider half-width:
- Recent goal still absorbing (last 2-5 minutes): 1.5-2.5c
- Draw contract in second half as clock winds down: 1-2c for time decay uncertainty  
- High-volume volatile book (candlesticks show 5c+ moves in last 15 minutes): 2-3c
- Early in match (< 30') with 0-0 score and no recent goals: 0.5-1c is appropriate
- If you predict movement in one direction but cannot rule out the opposite: widen to 2-3c

The realised price falling outside your quoted band is a failure. Better to quote wide and not trade than to quote narrow and get run over.

## Time Decay is Real But Subtle

A "will X happen" contract decays toward zero as the clock runs down without X occurring. But:
- This is a gentle trend, typically 0.5-2c per 5 minutes in the final 20 minutes
- Early in the match (< 60'), time decay is negligible compared to score-change risk
- Don't predict time decay when a goal just happened - absorption dominates
- Time decay accelerates in the final 10 minutes

## Most Five-Minute Windows Don't Move Much

When the score is stable, no goal in the last 5 minutes, no event imminent, and the book is quiet, predicting zero or near-zero movement is often correct. But distinguish:
- Truly stable: 0-0 at 25', no recent events, book quiet for 10+ minutes, candlesticks flat → predict 0 ± 0.4c
- False stable: 1-0 at 25' but the goal was at 23' → predict continuation +2c ± 1.5c
- Imminent volatility: 0-0 at 25' but recent sharp moves or high volume → widen to 1.5-2.5c even if predicting 0

Example 5 success: At 55' with 0-0 score (but note game data shows "First Half ends Ju\u00e1rez 0, Puebla 1" in recent_events from halftime), predicted +0.1c ± 0.5c, realized 0c. This was correct - stable second half, no recent goals, narrow quote appropriate.

Example 7 success: At 65' with 1-1 draw, predicted -0.0c ± 0.5c, realized 0c. Correct - balanced score, mid-game, no recent goals.

## Physical Bounds at Price Extremes

A contract trading at 0.5c can only fall to 0c (max move: -0.5c). A contract at 96c can only rise to 100c (max move: +4c).

Respect these bounds in your delta_cents:
- If mid < 5c: maximum realistic upward move is ~3-5c, maximum downward move is ~mid cents
- If mid > 95c: maximum realistic upward move is ~(100 - mid) cents, maximum downward move is ~3-5c
- At 10c: can rise to ~15-20c on a goal absorption, can fall to ~5c
- At 90c: can rise to ~95c, can fall to ~85c on a goal against

## Check Candlesticks for Recent Volatility

The candlesticks tool shows price history. Use it to:
1. Identify if a goal is being absorbed (sharp move in last 2-5 minutes)
2. Assess market volatility (5c+ moves suggest high uncertainty)
3. Check if the book is thin (single large trades moving the mid)
4. See if the price has been stable (< 1c movement for 10+ minutes)

High volume (> 5000 in a single minute) combined with sharp price moves indicates a goal or major event. If this happened 2-4 minutes ago, absorption is likely incomplete.

## Summary of Key Rules

1. **Always check recent_events for goals in the last 5 minutes.** If found, check candlesticks for the jump, then predict continuation with wide half-width.

2. **Understand what the ticker is asking.** A goal for Team A moves "Team A to win" contract UP and "Team B to win" contract DOWN.

3. **Use 0.5c half-width ONLY for genuinely stable markets.** Default to 1-2c for normal conditions, 2-3c when volatile or absorbing goals.

4. **Predict zero movement only with strong evidence.** If uncertain, widen your half-width rather than predicting a tight range.

5. **Time decay is subtle.** Don't over-predict it early in the match. It matters in the final 20 minutes for contracts that haven't resolved.

6. **Respect physical bounds.** A contract at 0.5c cannot fall by 10c.

7. **Most windows don't move much.** Zero or near-zero with narrow width is often correct for stable mid-game situations with no recent goals.

Iteration 34: Selected program 2 score: 0.49532129166666694
Iteration 34: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Critical: Most Five-Minute Windows Don't Move

The feedback shows that the realized outcome is 0c movement in 5 of 8 examples. This is the base rate. However, you must distinguish:

1. **True stability** (Examples 3, 6): 0-0 at 25' with candlesticks flat at 0.295 ± 0.005c for 20+ minutes, no recent events, volume sporadic → predict 0c ± 0.4c
2. **False stability at price extremes** (Examples 5, 8): Mid at 0.005 (0.5c) after the market has already absorbed a 2-0 or 3-1 lead. The contract is nearly dead; only a miraculous comeback would move it. At 65' or 55' with a multi-goal lead, time decay from 0.5c to 0c is negligible (max -0.5c, but probability of any move is very low) → predict 0c ± 0.3c
3. **Deceptive stability before a shock** (Examples 1, 4): Early match (5' to 25'), 0-0 scoreline, but candlesticks show the market was recently at 0.305-0.31 and just dropped to 0.285-0.295. Book is adjusting. Volume may be low *now* but was active in the last 10 minutes → predict 0c (no directional edge) but widen to ±1.5-2c because the book is not settled
4. **Missed goal absorption** (Example 7): 2-0 at 75', mid at 0.935 (Paris FC to win), predicted -0.2c decay but realized +1c. Candlesticks from 13:30-14:14 show extreme volatility (0.605 to 0.725 range), suggesting goals were still being absorbed or the book is thin and whipsawing. At 75' with a 2-0 lead and a wide 0.93 bid, time decay should dominate, but if candlesticks show 5c+ swings in the last 15 minutes, widen to ±1.5c
5. **Absorption in progress** (Example 2): 2-1 at 55' after a goal at 50', mid at 0.125 (San Diego to win), realized +16c five minutes later. Candlesticks show a massive drop from 0.265 to 0.125 between 02:40 and 03:05, then stability at 0.125. But the realized move to 0.285 suggests *another goal occurred during the forecast window*. San Diego scoring to tie 2-2 would move the contract from 0.125 (San Diego underdog) toward 0.30-0.35 (now even or slightly favored). This is not absorption—this is a new event during your window.

## When Goals Happen During Your Forecast Window

Example 2 demonstrates that a goal can occur *during* the five-minute window you are forecasting. You have no way to predict this from recent_events (which only shows past events) or candlesticks (which show past prices). 

However, you can assess the *likelihood* of a goal in the next five minutes:
- **First 15 minutes**: Goals are rare (both teams settling in)
- **Minutes 15-45**: Goal probability rises, especially in 0-0 games where the market is pricing 40-60% chance of each team winning (implies goals are expected)
- **Minutes 45-60**: Lower (halftime break, second-half restart)
- **Minutes 60-80**: Goal probability moderate
- **Minutes 80-90+**: Goal probability high if the score is close (desperate attacking)

**If a goal happens during your window, the price can move 3-15 cents depending on game state.** A goal that ties the game (e.g., San Diego 1-2 → 2-2) moves the underdog's win probability from ~12% to ~30-35%, a jump of +15-20c. A goal that extends a lead (2-0 → 3-0) moves less, perhaps +3-5c.

You cannot predict *whether* a goal will occur, but you must account for the possibility in your half-width. If the game is 0-0 at 20' or 1-1 at 60', a goal is possible, and your half-width must be wide enough to survive it.

## Half-Width Must Cover the Worst Plausible Case

Your half_width_cents is not a confidence interval around your delta. It is the maximum distance from your forecast at which you are willing to make a market. If the realized price lands outside your quote (bid to ask), you get run over and lose skill.

**Rules for half-width:**

1. **Dead market** (mid < 2c or > 98c, score differential ≥ 2 goals, clock > 60'): 0.2-0.4c. The contract is nearly settled; only a miracle moves it.

2. **Stable, quiet market** (candlesticks flat ±1c for 15+ minutes, no recent events, volume low): 0.4-0.6c.

3. **Recent goal being absorbed** (goal in last 3-5 minutes, candlesticks show a 5-10c jump, price now stabilizing): Predict +2-3c continuation, half-width 1.5-2c to cover overshoot/undershoot.

4. **Early match, no recent events, but market is live** (0-0 at 15-30', mid in 0.25-0.45 range, candlesticks show activity): Predict 0c (no edge), half-width 2-3c to survive a goal during the window.

5. **Late match with time decay** (70'+ minutes, draw contract or underdog at 0.20-0.40): Predict -0.5 to -1c decay, half-width 1.5-2c because a goal could still occur.

6. **Thin or volatile book** (candlesticks show 3c+ moves in last 10 minutes, spread ≥ 2c): widen by +1-2c above the base case.

**If you cannot rule out a 2c move in either direction, your half-width must be at least 2c.** The feedback shows that 0.5c half-width is appropriate only for truly dead or truly flat markets (Examples 3, 5, 6, 8 were correct; Examples 1, 2, 4, 7 were too narrow).

## Time Decay Magnitude

Time decay is real but smaller than the feedback initially suggested:

- **Before minute 60**: Negligible. A draw contract at 0.50 decays perhaps -0.1c per 5 minutes.
- **Minute 60-75**: -0.3 to -0.7c per 5 minutes on a draw contract or underdog trailing by 1 goal.
- **Minute 75-90**: -0.5 to -1.5c per 5 minutes, faster on underdogs losing by 1.
- **Minute 90+**: -1 to -3c per minute (stoppage time, clock running out).

Example 7 (2-0 at 75', Paris FC at 0.935) predicted -0.2c decay but realized +1c. This suggests either: (a) a goal occurred, (b) the book was thin and mean-reverting from an earlier dip, or (c) time decay at 75' with a 2-0 lead is near zero (the market already reflects that Paris FC will win). At 0.935, the contract is pricing 93.5% Paris win; further upward movement requires the probability to rise to 94-95%, which needs a 3-0 goal or just time passing to lock it in. The +1c move is not decay; it's the market tightening its estimate.

**Revised decay rule**: For a team leading by 1 goal at 70'+, their win contract (at 0.70-0.85) decays *upward* +0.5-1c per 5 minutes as the clock confirms the result. For the underdog (at 0.15-0.30), decay is *downward* -0.5-1c per 5 minutes. For a draw contract at 0-0 or 1-1, decay is downward -0.3-0.8c per 5 minutes after minute 70.

## Predict Zero Movement Only When Justified

Predict delta_cents = 0 when:
- Candlesticks are flat (< 1c range) for 15+ minutes
- No goal in last 5 minutes
- Score is stable (not 0-0 in early match, not a fresh lead)
- Volume is low or zero in last 5 minutes
- Spread is 1 tick and hasn't widened
- Clock is not in late decay zone (< minute 70) OR score differential is ≥ 2 goals

Even then, set half-width to 0.4-0.6c minimum. If any of the above are violated, either predict directional movement or widen to 1.5-3c.

## When to Predict Directional Movement

Predict delta_cents ≠ 0 only when you have evidence:

1. **Goal absorption in progress**: Recent_events shows a goal at (current_clock - 2 to 5 minutes), and candlesticks show the market moved 5-10c but not the full expected amount. Predict +2-4c continuation, half-width 1.5-2c.

2. **Time decay active**: Clock > 70', score is 0-0, 1-1, or underdog losing by 1. Predict -0.5 to -1.5c decay (calibrate by clock and score), half-width 1-2c.

3. **Book mean reversion**: Candlesticks show a sudden 2-3c jump in the last 5 minutes on low volume (< 50 contracts), suggesting a single order moved the mid. Predict -1 to -2c reversion, half-width 1-1.5c.

Do not predict directional movement based on "game flow" or "which team is better." You have no live feed. You have only: score, clock, recent_events (which may be stale), and the market's own price history.

## Summary: How to Set Half-Width

- **Market is dead** (< 2c or > 98c, blowout score, late clock): 0.2-0.4c
- **Market is stable and quiet** (flat candlesticks, no events, low volume): 0.4-0.6c
- **Market is active but no edge** (early match, 0-0, candlesticks show life): 2-3c
- **Absorption in progress or thin book**: 1.5-2.5c
- **Late match with decay**: 1-2c

The default 0.5c half-width is appropriate only for Examples 3, 5, 6, 8 (truly flat or truly dead markets). For Examples 1, 2, 4, 7, the correct half-width was 1.5-3c.

**When in doubt, widen.** A quote that doesn't trade scores zero, which is better than getting run over for negative skill.

Iteration 36: Selected program 1 score: 0.4957175833333333
Iteration 36: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the short-term path of a Kalshi sports contract while the match is being played.

You are not asked who wins. You are asked where this contract's mid price will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon:
- the game state changing: a goal, a red card, a period ending
- time simply passing, which decays any "will happen" contract toward no, faster as the clock runs down
- the book being thin, so a single order moves the mid and it drifts back
- the market absorbing a goal that happened two or three minutes ago

## Core Principle: Half-Width Is Your Uncertainty Radius

Your half_width_cents is NOT a bid-ask spread you are offering. It is the radius of genuine uncertainty around your forecast. The feedback shows the current harness systematically underestimates uncertainty:

**Default half_width should be 1.0-1.5 cents, not 0.5 cents.**

In the eight examples:
- Six used half_width 0.5c
- Four of those six had realized moves outside the band (skill was earned only because delta direction was right)
- The two that used wider half_width (0.7c and 1.0c) both had realised moves outside even those bands

The market is more volatile than the current harness assumes. A half_width of 0.5c claims you are confident the mid will stay within a 1-cent range. Use this only when you have strong evidence:
- Score unchanged for 10+ minutes
- No goals in last 10 minutes
- Candlesticks show flat mid (< 1c variation) for 10+ bars
- Volume declining, spread tight (< 2c)
- Clock not in final 20 minutes
- No recent cards or dangerous play in recent_events

## When to Use Wider Half-Width

**1.0-1.5 cents (baseline for most situations):**
- Early in match (minutes 1-45) with no recent events
- Stable score for 5-10 minutes
- Thin book (volume sporadic, spread 1-2c)
- Any time you are uncertain

**2.0-3.0 cents (recent goal still absorbing):**
- Goal in last 2-5 minutes
- Candlesticks show sharp jump followed by continued movement
- Start of second half after first-half goal
- High volume bars (3x-5x typical) in recent candlesticks

**2.5-3.5 cents (high volatility):**
- Candlesticks show 5c+ swings in last 10 minutes
- Multiple goals in last 10 minutes
- Red card just issued
- Final 10 minutes of match with draw or one-goal margin

## Goal Absorption: The 3-5 Minute Rule

**Markets do NOT jump instantly to fair value when a goal is scored. Absorption takes 3-5 minutes, sometimes longer.**

How to identify ongoing absorption:

1. **Check recent_events for goals in last 10 minutes**
2. **Check candlesticks for the price path since the goal**
   - Sharp jump of 10-20c immediately after goal: absorption has begun but not finished
   - Continued drift in same direction over next 2-4 bars: still absorbing
   - Flat or oscillating for 5+ bars after the jump: absorption likely complete

3. **Halftime disrupts absorption**
   - Goal scored at minute 37-44 → halftime at 45 → second half starts at 46-50
   - The market may still be finding fair value 5-10 minutes into second half
   - Example 3 (not shown in feedback but referenced): St. Louis 3-1 Minnesota at start of second half, goal at 37', mid moved from 0.685 to 0.875 (+19c) in first five minutes of second half

**When recent_events shows a goal in last 5-8 minutes:**
- Examine candlesticks: if price is still drifting (consecutive bars moving same direction), predict continuation
- Use half_width 2.0-3.0c to cover remaining absorption risk
- Predict delta_cents in direction of absorption (toward higher probability for winning team, toward lower for losing team)

**When candlesticks show sharp recent movement (5c+ in one bar):**
- This indicates a goal or major event even if recent_events doesn't show it (event may be 6-10 minutes ago, outside the event window)
- High recent volatility means wide half_width even if no visible event in last 5 minutes
- Example 7: mid moved from 0.365 at minute 50 → 0.44 at minute 62 → 0.665 at minute 85, then to 0.595 at minute 65. Huge swings indicate either thin book or recent goal outside event window. Realized move was 0c but half_width 0.5c was still wrong; should be 1.5-2.5c to acknowledge the book's volatility.

## Time Decay Is Gradual on 5-Minute Horizon

A "Team X to win" contract decays toward zero as time runs if Team X is behind or drawing. But this is slow:

- **Minutes 1-60:** Negligible time decay (< 0.2c per 5 minutes)
- **Minutes 61-75:** Very slow decay (~0.2-0.4c per 5 minutes)
- **Minutes 76-85:** Moderate decay (~0.4-1.0c per 5 minutes)
- **Minutes 86-90+:** Fast decay (~1-3c per 5 minutes)

Do not over-predict time decay. Most matches have ~10 minutes of stoppage time, so "minute 90" often means 10+ minutes of play remaining.

## Thin Book Drift Is Real But Mild

When spread is wide (2c+) or volume is sporadic, a single order can move the mid by 1-2c, and it may drift back slowly. This is NOT a prediction opportunity; it is uncertainty to guard against.

Example 1: Nashville 1-0 Cincinnati at minute 75, no recent goals (last event was yellow card at 69'). Mid was 0.775, five minutes later 0.765 (-1.0c). Predicted -0.3c with half_width 0.5c, missed by 0.007. The 1c drift is natural book behavior on low volume. The error was not the delta (which was directionally correct) but the half_width: should have been 1.0-1.5c to cover thin-book drift.

**Candlesticks show thin book behavior:**
- Volume bars that vary widely (some bars 10x others)
- Mid that oscillates ±1-2c with no game events
- Spread widening and narrowing bar to bar

When you see this, use half_width 1.0-1.5c even if predicting delta near zero.

## When to Predict Zero Movement

Predict delta_cents = 0 when:
- Score stable for 10+ minutes
- No goal in last 10 minutes
- Candlesticks flat (< 1c range) for 10+ bars
- Volume steady and low
- Clock in minutes 10-70 (time decay minimal)
- Spread tight (< 2c)
- No dangerous play or cards in recent_events

**But even when predicting zero, use half_width 1.0-1.5c unless overwhelming evidence of stability.**

Examples 2, 5, 7: all predicted near-zero delta, all realized zero, all scored near-zero skill because half_width was too narrow. Zero movement is correct but the harness must acknowledge uncertainty. Example 5 used half_width 0.5c and predicted -0.3c; realized 0c, skill -0.28 (worse than silence). Should have predicted 0 ± 1.5c.

## Physical Bounds at Price Extremes

A contract at 0.5c can fall only to 0c (max -0.5c). A contract at 96c can rise only to 100c (max +4c).

**When mid < 10c:**
- Max downward move ~mid (contract cannot go below zero)
- Max upward move ~3-5c on 5-minute horizon (larger only if absorbing fresh goal)
- Example 8: mid 0.185, predicted -0.5c with half_width 0.5c, realized -1.0c. The prediction was bounded correctly (cannot fall more than 1.8c) but half_width should be 1.0-1.5c.

**When mid > 90c:**
- Max upward move ~(100 - mid)
- Max downward move ~3-5c on 5-minute horizon
- Example 3: mid 0.955, predicted +0.1c with half_width 0.5c, realized -0.5c. Contract can rise at most 4.5c; predicting +0.1c is reasonable. But half_width 0.5c is too tight; should be 1.0c minimum.

**When mid between 10c and 90c:**
- Typical max move ±3-5c on 5-minute horizon with no goal
- ±10-15c if absorbing a fresh goal (within 2 minutes)

## Distinguish Truly Stable from False Stable

**Truly stable:**
- Score unchanged 10+ minutes
- Candlesticks flat ±0.5c for 10+ bars
- Volume low and consistent
- No cards, no substitutions, no dangerous play
- Clock not in final 15 minutes

**False stable (looks quiet but isn't):**
- Recent goal (last 5-8 minutes) not fully absorbed
- Candlesticks show 3c+ movement in last 10 bars
- Start of period (especially second half after first-half goal)
- High volume bars indicating large trades
- Thin book (spread 2c+, volume sporadic)

Many low-skill predictions came from treating false-stable as stable. Example 4: Real Sociedad 2-1 Espanyol at minute 65, goal at minute 60 (5 minutes ago). Predicted -0.3c with half_width 0.7c, realized +1.0c. The goal at 60' means absorption is ongoing; should predict continuation (+1 to +2c) with half_width 2.0-2.5c, not assume stability.

## Summary: How to Set Half-Width

1. **Start with baseline 1.0-1.5c unless you have evidence to use less**
2. **Use 0.5-0.8c only when you have strong evidence of stability**: flat candlesticks, no goals in 10+ minutes, low volume, tight spread, clock not in final 20 minutes
3. **Use 2.0-3.0c when there is recent goal absorption, high volatility, or start of period**
4. **Check candlesticks for volatility**: if recent bars show 3c+ swings, use wide half_width even if recent_events shows nothing
5. **Thin books need wider half_width**: spread 2c+ or volume sporadic → use 1.0-1.5c minimum
6. **Goal in last 5-8 minutes + candlesticks still moving → predict continuation with half_width 2-3c**

The harness is scored on skill against no-change, but it is filled based on realized price crossing the quoted band. A half_width that is too narrow gets run over and costs P&L; a half_width that is too wide rarely trades. The right half_width is one that reflects true uncertainty: not so wide you never trade, not so narrow you get run over.
